But I hate my body so much that it has been making me wildly depressed. I don’t want to go out and see anyone at all. I hate running into people I know in public, and I hate the fact that I start working in the school I did last year next week. I’ll have to see all of my co workers and the thought of that makes me want to crawl into a hole. It also takes all of the little will power I have left to continue to eat at all during my pregnancy. I am aware that me not eating is bad for my baby, but it is so hard to want to eat. I don’t have any cravings so that doesn’t help. I feel disgustingly guilty all of the time because of how negative I’ve been throughout my pregnancy as well. I didn’t work much over the summer so I’ve sat around drowning in my negative thoughts for three months. I can’t seem to get out of my own head about this and I am desperately needing this pregnancy to be over with. I don’t know how I’ve survived this long but I’m trying my best to push through until November. I’ve gotten to the point where my depression is the worst it has been in a very very long time. I’m drowning here and I don’t have anyone to talk to about it because I am entirely too embarrassed to even say anything to my boyfriend let alone anyone else. Sometimes I feel like I can’t make it to November to deliver this baby. I don’t know what to do or where to turn to anymore.

### 14. Work and benefits determine access to care now, consume leave meant for after birth and can penalize sickness or loss.

**Current findings section:** §8 leave/income; §10 work planner; §17.2 surviving the job now.

**Relationship to current conclusion: confirms and extends.**

Current wording: “the work posts are mostly about surviving the job now, not about leave dates.”

**What should change / interpretation:** Keep the wave-3 correction and add specific mechanisms: PTO delays the first prenatal visit, weekly IVs consume FMLA, mental-health treatment threatens an unprotected gap, and a boss explicitly counts stillbirth/pregnancy absences against performance. Include student/physical-job constraints and medical risk behind refused travel. Reported benefits and retaliation fears are not legal determinations.

**Verbatim evidence:**

It could be achieved. Finance wise, my partner works full time and I work 20 hours a week alongside being at uni, i also know there can be some support from the government, e’g £20 child benefit each week.

In short, I might have only a few years to have a baby without real heart issues.

**[pbu05-0268]**

> I have HG and am straight up not having a good time. I have no PTO left at work, and have to used FMLA to get to appointments. As my fellow Americans know, that will cut down on the time I'll have with baby after birth. I haven't told many people, as i was told I'd never have kids, and this is a high risk pregnancy. I need weekly IV appointments, so who knows if ill even have time off when the baby comes.
> 
> I am in survival mode, and am upset I haven't had time to enjoy anything about being pregnant.
> 
> I am just complaining at this point. I know stress is horrible for the body, and the baby. Did anyone have a super stressful pregnancy, and still have a healthy pregnancy and baby?

**[pbu05-0324]**

> I currently make twice the amount of money as my bf, I usually pay for almost everything including helping him with his rent once which never was paid back to me. Its fine, money isnt the object but the principal is as now its time to get serious ans save. This man doesnt even have a savings account. He lives alone with a pretty high car payment, lives paycheck to paycheck as a cook. I have an decent job with no degree but 12 years of experience in my field. I live with my brother who had a bad reaction to me getting pregnant which already put me in a bad place mentally. After expressing this to my bf he immediately tells me his little brother and he think I should start working at Costco. I was immediately furious (i admit pregnancy has made me reactive emotionally) and questioned why that could ever be a good idea as I currently make 30-40$/hour and only work 5-7 hour days (making it ideal for pregnancy). His response was that they have good health insurance and the birth of the baby will be cheap. This also made me furious as it implied that it is my responsibility to pay for our babies birth and to also think that I should give up on my career where I make twice the amount of money and also am so good at. I felt so invalidated to be told that I should change my career where I am already financially able to attain stability while he cant save a dime let alone take me on dates. I continued to question his reasoning and felt like it was sheer entitlement. He basically cut me off and got aggravated at me and hung up. Then proceeded to text me about how hes only trying to help. My response was, how can you suggest I change my job when I make twice the money as the job your suggesting and twice the amount that you make? Its infuriating. I also responded with, why not worry about you and your lack of ability to provide and even meet me halfway financially instead of worrying about what Im doing?

**[pbu05-0325]**

> I did tell him he needs to focus on getting his own sh** together. He clearly needs a second job in order to provide and save the next 7-8 months which he has brought up. But my problem is its all talk. I havent seen any effort put forward for him to apply and actively get another job. Meanwhile Ive been applying for housesitting and petsitting gigs on the side to make extra income.

**[pbu05-0328]**

> I graduated from law school in December. Was totally like the worst time of my life- I was pregnant for the last bit of it, but was diagnosed with a missed miscarriage on December 30. I did the whole process with the medication on January 3. I also lost two pets, and was supposed to sit for the February bar exam. Which I did, and failed. I expected it, but I was fine.

**[pbu05-0329]**

> Now I’m pregnant again, in my second trimester (finally) with my rainbow baby. I was supposed to retake the bar exam this week, today and tomorrow to be exact. But once I found out I was pregnant this time, I just hard stopped studying. As mostly every pregnant woman knows, you have no motivation. You’re exhausted. And everything is a chore. So obviously my discipline did not overcome the first trimester trenches, and I fell way behind.
> 
> Now, with today being exam day, I’ve explained to pretty much everyone under the sun that I’ve postponed my exam- some knowing the exact reason while others are just left to speculate that I just didn’t study enough or I gave up. I have to postpone to July of next year- I can’t imagine that I wanna sit in February after giving birth three weeks prior. I probably wouldn’t even get accommodations lol. But I’ve just had a lot of negative feedback. 
> 
> People are telling me that I’m putting my life on hold, I’m gonna be too tired with a baby that I just pushed back bar prep for 2 to 3 years. My own mom is like I just hope that you see your lot agree through… It’s like the lack of confidence in my career goals- it’s  just crazy now that I’m pregnant. 
> 
> I feel like it’s a valid reason? Yeah I was careless about the timeline, but the timeline never really made a difference to me. Like at the end of the day, I know I’ll be an attorney. But do I really want to be an attorney at 26? Do you know what I mean? I’m just so tired of like other people’s agendas.
> 
> **Looking to speak with other women who also experienced doubts, but still became a badass mama in her career.**

**[pbu05-0413]**

> 1. Would you go back to work now, or wait until after giving birth? If I waited, it would be about 1.5 years between leaving my job and returning. I could also potentially go back part-time if that made more sense.
> 
> 2. When would you disclose the pregnancy? If I reach out now, should I tell them that I'm pregnant upfront, or wait until I'm farther along? If I asked to go back part-time I feel like I would need to tell them why up front.  Obviously, they'll be able to do the math eventually and realize I was already about 3 months pregnant when I returned. I don't want them to feel like I was being deceptive by not mentioning it.
> 
> A few additional factors, this is considered a high-risk pregnancy, and there's a possibility I may need to stop working around 32 weeks, although I won't know that for sure until I'm farther along. Financially, my husband and I *could* manage without me working until after the baby is born, but we'd need to use some of my inheritance for day-to-day expenses and baby preparations. My current health insurance is through the Marketplace and is expensive, so going back to work would potentially help financially with that as well.
> 
> I would be eligible for maternity leave regardless of how long I'd been back at the company.
> 
> I'm torn between wanting to get back to work and wanting to give myself as much flexibility as possible during the pregnancy. I'd really appreciate hearing what others would do in this situation.

### 15. Care navigation includes getting records corrected, referrals acted on, measurements explained and someone to respond when symptoms change.

**Current findings section:** §5 being believed and follow-up; §17.4 care that explains nothing.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends.**

Current wording: “A 'what your appointments should cover and what to ask at each' page answers both.”

**What should change / interpretation:** Keep the question list but qualify the implication that it answers the gap: parents also meet unavailable appointments, missed referrals, lost history, conflicting measurements, blame and medicines without guidance. Some need an interpreter, another clinician or escalation rather than a better-prepared question. Preserve helpful care and differences between staff within the same service.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu05-0019]**

> So basically what I’m dealing with right now is that my BP and HR is very high at the Doctors office so they are obviously freaking out . However, medical settings are my number 1 trigger and almost always cause me a panic attack. I have tried to communicate this to them but they don’t listen.

**[pbu05-0055]**

> I went to my regular OB today and, once again, they took my BP with my arm dangling down at my side, unsupported. It came back 142/89.
> 
> I told them, “I want my arm supported.”
> 
> They retook it with my arm properly supported, and it was 123/83.
> 
> A 19-point difference in systolic pressure simply from changing the position of my arm. And this isn't the first time I've seen this happen. And one even acknowledged that it should be supported. Okay so why does staff continue to not follow strict guidelines when it comes to blood pressure reading?!?!

**[pbu05-0109]**

> My last 3 readings at the midwife have been something like 135/92, so they were obviously concerned. Ever since I had a high first reading at 8w I began monitering my blood pressure at home, all have been in the range of 110-120/70-80.
> 
>   
> I mentioned this to my midwife, and they said they can't take those readings into account because it's not an NHS calibrated machine. Ok fair enough. They then said they can't give me a machine to take home because they had none available, so my only option was to measure my blood pressure in my GP's waiting room. If it's still high for a few weeks then I'll be started on meds.
> 
>   
> I did this on Monday and again my anxiety was through the roof using a loud machine in front of everyone. Got a high reading.
> 
>   
> So now I have no idea if my machine at home is just wrong or if it's the anxiety. I obviously want whatever is best for the health of my baby, if I really do have high blood pressure then of course I'll start medication. My worry is that I might not have high blood pressure day to day and if I start medication then I risk the side effects (which my midwife explained) to both me and baby.

**[pbu05-0125]**

> I’m 32+5 and had a hospital appointment today (consultant appointment to book in c-section) where they took a routine urine sample. When I got home, I had a look at my maternity notes and saw they had included a vial with my name written on it, with what I expect is my urine sample (having been transferred from the pot they gave me). I tried to call them and my midwife line but it was after 5pm and no one answered, so I sent an email which hopefully they’ll respond to in the morning (and I will call them again if not).
> 
> However, I think went back to read the notes as I realised I hadn’t checked them after the confusion of finding the urine sample. For all my previous samples, they have written “NAD”, but for this one they wrote “Ery +4”. ChatGPT says this means a “strongly positive finding of blood” in the urine. About a month ago, I went in with some bad stomach pains and had to do a urine sample, and 2 weeks later they called me to say I’d had a UTI and to start a 7 day course of antibiotics. I then got asked to drop off a urine sample a week after finishing that course of antibiotics, which I did early last week and have heard nothing back. I’m wondering if anyone else has had a similar experience and if this means the UTI is still there?

**[pbu05-0126]**

> I’ll obviously speak to them again in the morning but just wondering about other’s experiences given I have to wait until the morning to speak to someone! I expect they’ll just ask me to go back and do another sample tomorrow and then wait another 2 weeks to hear back from that! It’s a pain they sent me home with my sample and didn’t mention any issues during the consultant appointment - I assume she didn’t read the notes or she’d have seen the sample that was folded into that page.

**[pbu05-0128]**

> I’ve had three incredibly devastating late losses and I’m finding myself increasingly anxious about the care I’m receiving during this pregnancy. I’ve had several experiences with my midwifery team that have left me feeling unheard, particularly when it comes to acknowledging my history of stillbirths and losses and birth preferences.   
> It feels as though this is consistently overlooked in conversations despite how significant it is to my care.  
> At my appointment today I was concerned to find that a needle was reused twice during a blood draw. My blood pressure was also taken incorrectly at an earlier appointment. After everything we’ve been through these experiences have really shaken my confidence in the NHS.  
> Following our losses, I was placed on the postnatal ward alongside women who had just given birth to healthy babies which was one of the most traumatising events in my life. The bereavement midwife assured me this wouldn’t happen again but I was given the same assurance previously and it happened again. I appreciate that these situations can be complicated and they have to prioritise other women for private rooms but the impact of being placed in that environment after such tragic losses was enormous.

**[pbu05-0212]**

> From the very first scan (around 4 weeks), our doctor's attitude felt off. Before even doing the ultrasound, she was already planting seeds of doubt. After the scan she said everything looked fine, then immediately followed it with "don't worry if you bleed in the next couple of days." No explanation, no warmth and she just left the room.
> 
> Second scan a week later, same energy. She walked in and said "we hope to see some improvement, if not it's an unhealthy pregnancy" before even scanning. She saw the yolk sac, mentioned it almost as an aside, and told us to come back in a week hoping to hear a heartbeat, "if not the baby is unhealthy." :(

**[pbu05-0213]**

> *No heartbeat detected.* The doctor's face actually seemed to lighten up at that point. She never congratulated us but that day she said "I'm sorry" multiple times with a lot of confidence, told us to expect bleeding soon, and prescribed medication to bring to the next scan a week later if the baby doesn't pass naturally.   
>   
> When I asked if it was confirmed, she said she didn't want to say 100% but that at 6w3d there should be a heartbeat.

**[pbu05-0254]**

> So I freak out, wondering how this could have happened. I immediately go to an urgent care clinic, wait 4 hrs, and then see a doctor. She looks down there, says that the strings are hanging very low and it looks like my iud how been pretty much hanging out of me for who knows how long. So she yanks it out. She tells me there might be some spotting for a few days, and then sends me on my way. No other instructions.
> 
> 3 days later on a Monday, I start having brown discharge, looked like old blood. And just a little bit every now and then. On Friday after work, it starts bleeding, quite a bit. So I go to the ER (Saskatoon RUH sucks) and basically wait there for 5 hours crying cause I think I’m having a miscarriage. I overhear another lady that said she had been there for 12 hrs, and decide that I would rather have a miscarriage at home than in that dirty waiting room surrounded by strangers and staff that dont really seem to care.

**[pbu05-0255]**

> At my family doctors I see a nurse practitioner in the lobby who pretty much tells me that I’m having a miscarriage and to just go home as there’s nothing they can do. I believed her as that is what I thought, but I decided to go to my urgent care appointment anyway as maybe they could confirm it. 
> 
> I go to that appointment and the doctor there is amazing!! So caring and helpful and he ordered me bloodwork and an ultrasound. 
> 
> I get bloodwork done and go for an ultrasound two days later. Since I got bloodwork in the ER, I was able to get the follow up bloodwork done, and it showed that my HCG levels were still rising! When I went to the ultrasound they didn’t see anything wrong, but it was still too early to make out the baby or hear a heart beat. But this all gave us hope! The urgent care doctor followed up with me and ordered another ultrasound for the next Friday, at this one we were able to hear a heartbeat and see the baby! 
> 
> Two weeks later I went for a 3rd ultrasound for an official dating ultrasound and the bag has grown a bunch and has a solid heartbeat! So I am currently just over 9 weeks now with a healthy baby! 
> 
> So to sum it up, yes you can get pregnant with the copper iud (so beware if you have it) and I guess you can bleed a lot pregnant and it can still turn out okay 🥹

**[pbu05-0260]**

> I’m already crying at this point and it’s worse now that they separated me and my husband. I tell them as best as I can that I just found out I was pregnant and I have dark brown discharge. I have PCOS/PMOS and got pregnant on birth control and while taking tirzepatide. Without scanning or taking blood, they insinuate I’m having a miscarriage and it’s my fault because of the meds I was taking even though I stopped taking them as soon as I found out I was pregnant. I try to tell them I also have two cysts on my right ovary and I just wanted to make sure our baby was okay and that pain was just from those cysts (one is a dermoid cyst). No one listened to my medical history or the fact that I mentioned I may need vaginal progesterone because I have low progesterone and my birth control was progesterone only. I also explained I got that I was 5 weeks based on a tracking app so I didn’t know if it was accurate. They never measured or confirmed where I was actually at. 
> 
> They finally do a transvaginal ultrasound and they only focus on the cysts. They do one view of the sac. There’s a yolk in there also and it looks like baby is developing well. They still tell me I will miscarry and tell me to follow up in 3 days. This is right when there was a national holiday, so even though they put the referral request in, no one was even there for the next 2 days to get me scheduled in, and they marked me as high risk, high priority.

**[pbu05-0261]**

> I get to the appointment (it was a nightmare finding parking because so many people were there after the holiday). I waited longer than the wand was inside of me. They locate the cysts, didn’t say anything about the baby other than there were actually 2 sacs, and told me to expect the miscarriage because they didn’t have anything to compare the scans to. They didn’t keep record of my ER visit. Then they scheduled me to come back next week to confirm that my body expelled everything. 
> 
> Immediately after my appointment, I went to pick up my husband for an appointment he had at a different medical clinic. They were much nicer and had a much more involved interpreter. The one at the hospital I went through did not speak to me unless the doctor or nurse told her something. 
> 
> This new place scheduled me for HCG (after my sweet husband spoke up for me, I was almost catatonic after being bluntly told that I’m miscarrying) and I did that appointment today (less than 24 hours of meeting them). They stayed with me the entire time. I hear back on Monday and the sweet nurse that scheduled me originally made sure to tell me that I would heard from her directly.

**[pbu05-0262]**

> I still go in next Thursday for another ultrasound at the hospital I do not like. They said they’re going to push for meds to help me abort. 
> 
> My husband keeps saying he has this gut feeling and not to listen to the doctors at the other hospital. Our plan this weekend is to keep going like I’m still pregnant until we can confirm that my HCG is going down. But I’m mentally prepared for the worst. 
> 
> I’m pissed, I’m sad, I feel helpless and hopeful all at the same time. I never thought I’d be able to conceive naturally and, if anything, I’m glad to know that IT IS POSSIBLE. I have the best husband I could’ve possibly asked for and he helps me move through all my feelings. He doesn’t invalidate me or make me feel crazy. I want to hold onto hope, especially because there’s so many ways this can end positively. But my anxiety is debilitating at this point because of the way everything has been handled up to this point and I just needed to get this all out somewhere.

**[pbu05-0346]**

> I’m 23 weeks with my first and had light, dark colored spotting last night/into this morning. I wasn’t super worried but figured I’d give my OB’s office a call today since I had previous bleeding at 10 weeks due to a subchorionic hematoma. My OB’s office advised me to go to OB triage so I did.
> 
> I let the nurse and midwife and the hospital know that I have a low-lying anterior placenta when they were hooking up monitors and was honest when they asked if I recently had sex. They both looked at me absolutely stunned and asked if my doctor told me not to have sex because of my placenta’s position. Other than a two week period after being diagnosed with the hematoma, my doctor has mentioned several times that I’m cleared for any usual activities, specifically mentioning sex as being safe.
> 
> I never thought to ask after my anatomy scan when they learned about my placenta’s position if that was still okay though. I feel dumb for not asking and not knowing that could hurt my baby so I definitely will not be having sex again until I’m cleared after birth. Also, I definitely caused my family to panic because I told them my doctor advised me to go to the hospital. Now I’m not quite sure what to tell them. We’re a “no such thing as TMI” family but I personally draw the line when it comes to sex… especially when it’s the first time when I had sex while pregnant.
> 
> So word of advice for the girlies with low-lying anterior placentas… make sure you check with your providers *after* your anatomy scan before you have sex!😅

**[pbu05-0354]**

> Just had my first appointment with the midwife! My first appointment was with the OB at around 8 weeks. Today I am 13 weeks 4 days. I came in with a list of questions/worries and she definitely reassured me with her medical advice/knowledge. About to go get some more blood drawn. Hopefully my ferritin levels have went up since I started taking an extra supplement. 🤞🏻 My blood pressure is good but sometimes anxiety causes my diastolic to be in in the high 80s/low 90s. They checked it at the end of the appointment and it was great. She wants me to start a baby aspirin and we’ll keep an eye on the blood pressure with office visits and at home monitoring. She used a doppler at the end of the appointment and his heart rate is 155bpm. I’m so happy that she seems so knowledgeable and helpful. Just excited that my baby boy is doing well and that I can put some of my worried to rest!

**[pbu05-0414]**

> Anyway, last night, about 2 hours before my medication was due, my blood pressure was 180/100. I was not doing anything, I took this manually while sitting on the couch. I even had my husband double check it because of how high it was reading. So, I took my Labetolol a little early and laid down for 10 minutes. Rechecked it, still saying 175/100 so I decided to go to the ED. 
> 
> Upon arrival to the ED, 30 minutes had passed since I took my meds so my blood pressure had gone down to 153/91. Transferred up to L&D (now an hour since I took the Labetolol) and my BP went down again: 128/78. 
> 
> I'm getting frustrated (I know it's almost over) because EVERY single TIME I go to L&D my blood pressure normalizes and the doctors/nurses look at me like I'm a lunatic. I feel like the boy who cried wolf. I keep ending up there after I already take my Labetolol. I was literally just there earlier this week because the doctor told me to go in. I just called to get a note to start maternity leave because my BP gets high when I do literally anything (standing).

**[pbu05-0415]**

> I call and report my blood pressure readings just to be safe and they tell me to go to L&D. So, I go. Then I get there and the BP cuff is on so loose that my husband could quite literally fit almost his entire hand inside the cuff. And this happens every single time! I shake my arm or move my arm down and the cuff literally slides off of my arm. 
> 
> I get there and my blood pressure is magically better and the doctor is snarky. I ask her what I need to do to avoid my blood pressure from spiking when I move around, no advice. Her advice was "Maybe take your blood pressure a little less". Um?? Ma'am?? That's not an answer?? Then she says my blood pressure is "Great" and my Labetolol is working "Great" but again, nothing about how to avoid these spikes. She does acknowledge that my dose is wearing off but since I'm almost due, there's no point in adding Procardia (which I agree with). I swear to god she thinks I'm a lunatic.
> 
> I was never someone who went to the doctor 24/7 before becoming pregnant. Now I end up on the L&D floor literally every single week. And every time the cuff is loose AF on my arm and my blood pressure is fine.

### 16. Parents facing a diagnosis want a matching case, a picture of everyday life and an explanation of what another test would change.

**Current findings section:** §4 attempts to understand choices; §5 professionals; §6 exact asks; §10 personalization.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends.**

Current wording: “with my specific needs not just numbers”

**What should change / interpretation:** Make personalization concrete: a particular translocation, MSUD daily care, cleft feeding/surgery, CDH at birth, maternal treatment and conflicting screening. Include the burden and uncertain usefulness of invasive testing, religious conflict and decisions about continuation. A risk percentage or Punnett square may leave the practical question unanswered. The educational clinical case is advisor material, not independent parent success.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu11-0027]**

**[pbu11-0028]**

No comment on health or how they look, just "there it is". 
> 
> She orders the blood test, we get it done, and then obviously we leave feeling mortified. We had no idea we were walking into a bomb like that. We go home and are crushed, only to get the bloodtest back and see that my wife's levels decreased EVEN MORE. ALMOST NORMAL. She then writes in the notes all of the doom and gloom she told us but at the end puts - no significant concerns; which pretty much negates the entirety of the visit.

**[pbu11-0144]**

> I wish I could sit here and say it wouldn’t impact my decision to have other children but it would. I was studying children’s nursing before I got pregnant and i’ve worked in many wards, hospices and community nursing programs which means i’ve saw a lot and how things like this affect other children and the family. I simply don’t know if I could risk it or at the very least be caught off guard by something like this. I feel like by getting tested we’re violating my SILS wishes and privacy because I know if my partner does come back with something we would tell his parents, get my daughter tested and deal with the impacts it would have on expanding our family.

**[pbu11-0211]**

> The CVS was awful because I had a tricky placenta, so they had to clamp my cervix, reposition multiple times, and it took awhile to obtain a sample. The ultrasound tech was getting overwhelmed and the Dr had to remove my sheet so she could see where she was because of the tech's inability to communicate her position. I was uncontrollably shaking afterward.
> 
> Not only that, but I didn’t receive any results or communication for two weeks because of lab issues and because the genetic counselor had a family emergency. The worst part was the bleeding. I had zero spotting before the CVS, then 24 hours later I started bleeding constantly, all the colors, including gushes of bright red blood. It didn’t taper off until 12 days later, and even now, a month later, I still get random bursts of spotting.
> 
> Then this experience somehow got even worse. I showed up for my follow-up appointment after waiting 2 weeks and apparently there had been a breakdown in communication. I have NO RESULTS and apparently never will because the sample wasn’t usable. I saw a decent chunk in the test tube, so I have no idea what happened. All of this suffering and ongoing side effects were for nothing.

**[pbu11-0212]**

I’m honestly traumatized by the CVS experience, and although I know amnios are generally tolerated better, I don’t know if I can mentally put myself through another invasive procedure for an outcome I can’t change.
>

>

**[pbu11-0293]**

> I am 40 and 14w pregnant with my first baby after a missed miscarriage and an early miscarriage. I also suffer from cPTSD and anxiety. I naturally overthink everything given the mc experiences. I had a private NIPT done which did not flag up any issues, however the combined screening test with NHS (UK) indicated high risk (1:127) for DS, mainly due to my age. I had previously felt reassured about the NIPT but ever since I received the phone call from the hospital I’m in a permanent state of panic. I can now chose to get another NIPT or CVS. I can also wait 2 weeks and get amniocentesis. I don’t know what to do and my anxiety is so severe I am unable to think straight. Having second reassuring NIPT would probably be enough for most people but my fear is that the anxiety will not stop and I will still panic about being that 1% with false negative. I am also terrified that if things went wrong because of the invasive test I’d never forgive myself. I know that nobody can tell me what to do and only I can make that decision but if someone could share their stories or input it would be very helpful. Please go gentle on me, I am genuinely immobilised with fear and struggle to think rationally. I don’t want to harm my baby but equally I don’t want to risk it. 

**[pbu11-0307]**

> My mind immediately started to swirl. I was so caught off guard that I didn't even know what questions to ask. Of every situation I'd envisioned, never in a million years was this one of them.
> 
> We got in the car and I broke down. I cried for the rest of the day. I couldn't even tell my family about our anatomy scan results, which they were eagerly anticipating. "10 fingers and 10 toes" is something you grow up hearing in delivery rooms as an indication of a healthy baby, and my baby only had nine fingers. She wasn't even born yet and I was failing her.
> 
> I started to beat myself up over how I felt about her limb difference, like I shouldn't have been that upset. It felt ableist and petty. And those thoughts just made me feel worse and made me feel like a terrible mom. I didn't love her any less, but I was terrified of what her life would look like. I found some remarkable stories about kids with the same condition that lessened my fear, but then I read others about what they had endured from other people. What other little kids had said about their hands. Called them weird. Ugly. My heart broke for my baby girl over scenarios that hadn't even happened.
> 
> And then there was the fear of what had caused these two anomalies. There was no way of knowing whether they were related or not, so I spent the remainder of my pregnancy worrying about what outcome we'd have at the other end.

**[pbu11-0308]**

> Ultimately, I developed severe polyhydramnios and had a uterine rupture at 36 weeks. Thankfully, after a relatively uneventful C-section, my beautiful daughter was born. It was quickly discovered that she had esophageal atresia and TEF, and she was taken to the NICU. Her esophagus and trachea were connected abnormally, and her esophagus wasn't connected to her stomach. Additionally, her stomach wasn't connected to her intestines, something called duodenal atresia.
> 
> She spent 148 days in the NICU and had five major surgeries there, and another two a few months later. She's still primarily feeding-tube fed, and we still have our challenges, but what I was so scared of two years ago isn't one of them. Her arm is one of the most beautiful things about her. There is not a single thing she cannot do. When giving high-fives, she'll use her lucky fin. She'll easily carry and manipulate items in the crook of her arm. She climbs after her big brother.

### 17. A birth plan needs workable pain-relief and communication contingencies; flexibility does not explain failed analgesia, rushed change or missing debrief.

**Current findings section:** §4 preferences/pain/consent; §6 fear; §10 plan B.

**Relationship to current conclusion: confirms and extends.**

Current wording: “Flexibility alone does not explain a good or difficult birth.”

**What should change / interpretation:** The restoration holds. Add low platelets removing an expected epidural option, vaginismus before an epidural, rapid dilation preventing requested medication, unbearable pain despite extra dosing and surgery followed by immediate feeding demands. Good rapid theatre care and missing follow-up can occur in one birth. Keep coping tools distinct from promises of an outcome.

> My doctor offered me two induction dates, 39 weeks or 40 weeks. I know for a fact I do not want to go over 40 weeks because still birth is my BIGGEST fear. I told her I would prefer 39 weeks.

> \-going past 40 weeks increases risk of stillbirth but only by a small percentage  
> \-I want a natural birth without pitocin and I’m afraid of the “cascade of interventions”  
> \-half of what I’ve read said babies lungs and brain are fully developed by 37 weeks where the other half says the last week is important for development   
> \-terrified of the risk of emergency c-section if baby goes into distress due to pitocin contractions  
> \-what if my due date is wrong and I get induced too early?

### 18. Pregnancy number cannot stand in for surviving children, time since birth, previous injury, future fertility or current caregiving capacity.

**Current findings section:** §9 segments; §10 personalization/older-child plan; §§16.5 and 17.3 pregnancy-number selector.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends and contradicts assumptions inside earlier selector advice.**

Current wording: “Pregnancy number alone remains too thin a selector.”
Additional current wording to correct: “Second-time content must assume she has a child to mind”

**What should change / interpretation:** Retain this restored conclusion and correct the earlier assumption that a second-time reader necessarily has one older child. Ask about loss history, surviving children, interval and recovery, other children's ages, transport, help and previous outcomes. VBAC can mean holding a toddler, travelling to unfamiliar care or avoiding loss of control; a third birth may require 400-mile separation and discussion of hysterectomy. A later pregnancy can also be easier.

> I am 14 weeks along into my second pregnancy. All the way through the first trimester, and I can say with confidence that I had a much easier time with it. Sure, I’ve had a couple rough days here and there but it does not hold a candle to my first. And the second I turned 14 weeks, any nausea I did have completely went away! Last time, it didn’t fully fizzle out until 20 weeks. I’m just so glad it was easier this time. So for anyone who is having a rough go around with their first and are wondering if you can even handle a second one, I wanted to offer some reassurance. 💕

### 19. Maternal recovery and the baby's hospital course can separate; feeding and connection can start well, break down and later improve.

**Current findings section:** §2 recovery/feeding/bonding; §4 preparation; §6 first-weeks fears.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends.**

Current wording: “The first weeks include incapacity, feeding pressure and delayed connection.”

**What should change / interpretation:** Keep that finding and add sequence: an emergency section followed by transfer, inability to hold the baby, illness after discharge/readmission, lack of lactation support and pumping competing with scarce time holding a hospitalized baby. Connection is not always absent initially; it can fade after a good first week. Preserve positive retrospectives and authors' practices as attributed experience.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu11-0082]**

> Our nurse was not helpful in telling me what to do but very rudely kept telling me he was not eating enough, if I couldnt do it correctly he would not be able to leave the hospital, and in general did not have any patienc eor understanding. I second guessed myself and decided not to request another nurse because i thought maybe my hormones were making me overthink until the next day when my husband said he had also noticed she was very snappy to me. Do not be afraid to ask to switch nurses! Yes, it is important for baby to learn to eat of course, but birth is very hard and no one should be making you feel like you are failing. The next day all of our nurses said i was doing everything right, he just was too relaxed and needed to constantly be wiggled a bit to stay awake.

**[pbu11-0083]**

> We went home the next day, which is something I now regret. I wish I would have stayed just for that extra support, but I couldn't have foreseen truly how bad the second night syndrome would be. Our son screamed so bad, and I could tell he was hungry but he just would not stay latched. Then our power went out, and it was so very hot. I was afraid he would overheat, and was not aure what amount of layers would be appropriate at all. They say one layer more than you wear, but i run VERY cold and wear sweaters when other people would usually just be in a shirt. The weekend he was born, the rest of our household was on a trip out of town, so it was just me, my husband and baby. The power came on and panicked in the middle of the night, i unthinkingly put the collustrom i had pumped in the microwave. It exploded and i lost everything i had saved.

**[pbu11-0084]**

> We immediately went to the ER, and after waiting an hour, a doctor came in and said he had a bilirubin level of 25, qnd we would need to be flown out to another hospital. I was so terrified, I didn't know what questions to ask. I knew jaundice was very common, but I had never heard of a baby having it this badly. I knew what jaundice did, my husband mother the year previously passed from the combination of cancer and jaundice attacking her liver. This experience massively set off post partum anxiety for me.

When physician arrived babies heart rate dipped again while being removed and he needed support for about 1-2 minutes before breathing on his own. He was whisked away to the NICU, I haven’t been able to hold him. I could tell things were serious based on reactions from the drs, anesthesiologists and nurses. I even saw some crying.

**[pbu05-0158]**

> At 19 weeks in-utero our daughter was diagnosed with Tetralogy of Fallot (TOF). We were told that it is the most common congenital heart defect, and that despite requiring surgery she would be able to live a happy and normal life. There may be some challenges and bumps along the way, but overall this would not be a problem if all things considered stayed within a tolerance. We have another happy and healthy 2 year old so, while this news was devastating, my wife and I were able to lean on each other for support and had a 2 year old reminder of all the good things in life.
> 
> At 39+5 my daughter was delivered by c-section and taken to the NICU for monitoring. Thankfully she was released to my wife in the maternity ward after 24 hours and my wife and daughter discharged from hospital after 48 hours.
> 
> We’d seen the cardiologist at 2 weeks old and were told that, while her heart anatomy showed that her affected artery was on the smaller side, he was quite happy with her growth and would be seeing us in 8 weeks time.

**[pbu05-0159]**

> After only 6 weeks, my daughter began to rapidly decline. Crying constantly and having frequent blue spells. This all culminated when we called the paramedics after she had refused to feed for 12 hours and was extremely lethargic.
> 
> In hospital, my daughter further declined and was having frequent blue spells. Her oxygen was dropping low even with minor exertions such as wriggling around. She had a significant blue spell over the weekend which caused a “code blue” to be called and all hands on deck. It was the scariest moment of my life, and the worst day of my life that day also, seeing my little one so scared and distressed and being able to do nothing to calm her.
> 
> She had surgery yesterday. Open heart surgery. It was a successful TOF repair, however her artery required a lot of work and unfortunately she was left with a slightly leaky valve. We have been told this is common for TOF repairs on such young children at 2 months old.
> 
> Our daughter is a fighter, but only through the grace of god. I could never wish the heartache, pain and trauma of this whole experience on any individual. To see your child in such a situation feels entirely helpless and lonely despite all the love and support around you. Seeing my wife crushed and broken, and being completely unable to heal her broken heart has made me feel so worthless as a husband, but we communicate well after-the-fact and she has reassured me that she sees each effort I put in and she loves and appreciates me. She’s reminded me that, as much as I am there to support her, she supports me also.

**[pbu05-0195]**

> She was 4lbs 4oz, bigger than they expected so that was nice to hear. I only seen her for less than a minute before she was wheeled to the nicu. I was hooked up to magnesium for 24 hours after and couldn’t see my baby until the 24 hour mark. The first time I seen her I couldn’t hold her since she had to stay under a blue light for jaundice. I full 48 hours after she was ripped from my womb I got to hold her… I sadly felt nothing but fear and panic the entire time she was in the nicu… 27 days

**[pbu05-0374]**

> \- **Don’t feel guilty if you suddenly lose all the love for your baby.** I was in love with mine for a week after delivery, but then the hormones came. And broken sleep. And my husband going crazy. And colics, god the colics. Thankfully they stopped after switching to hypoallergenic formula but I still had to return to Zoloft after 3 weeks of not wanting to be around my child at all. I didn’t want to touch him or be near him. All I could think of was how I’ve ruined my life because now I’m stuck in this sleepless nightmare with a little worm that doesn’t even know it exists. I finally saw the light at 6 weeks when he smiled for the first time. I finally thought to myself this is doable and I will be alright. Thankfully this hard time passed and now I want to be around him 24/7 and I can’t get enough of that little ball of sunshine.  
> \- **Don’t feel guilty if you don’t love breastfeeding or pumping.** I always wanted to BF but my son stayed in NICU for 7 days and I wasn’t even able to try, but pumping **destroyed me.** I was a shell of myself and constantly in pain. I couldn’t sleep and rest while he slept because I needed to pump. I was crying so bad because I was only able to be with him for couple of hours a day and I’ve wasted so much of it on pumping instead of holding him. I wanted to stop so bad but medical staff was forcing me to continue cause “it’s best for the baby”. I cried and was telling them I want to spend time with a baby not with a machine, and they told me I’ll have plenty of time to be with him in the future and that working on milk supply is worth it. Fuck that. If your baby has to choose between milk and mentally stable mom, you know what it’d prefer!

**[pbu05-0375]**

> In the end I wanted to let you know that I think my baby being exclusively formula fed might be making a huge difference in how chill my experience was so far. My husband always takes one night feeding (he did this even in his murder phase) so I end up with only one night time waking, which now lasts only 10 minutes to feed and nappy change. I can also give him to my MIL or parents or anyone really without issues, because my boobs are not needed.
> 
> Son also sleeps on his stomach -don’t come at me. He started doing this at 3 weeks when colics hit and we got a green light from our paediatrician since he was able to lift and turn his head without issues. We were always keeping him near us, plain mattress, nothing in his crib, high quality, dense and elastic sheet. Now he’s able to roll himself to and from belly so we let him sleep however he likes. I’m only mentioning this because I’ve heard that babies sleep a lot deeper while on their stomach (no startle reflex) so it might be one of the reasons why he’s such a good sleeper. The first three weeks he was sleeping on his side, we were switching them after every feed. He never once slept on his back outside of a carrier, he just doesn’t like it so we don’t fight it.

### 20. Recovery is physical incapacity, bleeding, body-image distress and sexual boundaries as well as supplies; a previous recovery does not predict this one.

**Current findings section:** §2 postpartum recovery; §5 partner support; §6 body and identity.

**Relationship to current conclusion: confirms and extends.**

Current wording: “Recovery is not just a supplies checklist;”

**What should change / interpretation:** The restoration is confirmed. Add the gap between a 5-5-5 agreement and doing household labor, persistent bleeding despite assessment, inability to climb stairs, anemia, distress at four weeks or later, and pressure for sex despite pain. A very helpful father can still keep asking for sex. Preserve reports of improvement and easy newborn sleep beside difficult accounts.

**Verbatim evidence:**

> I am 13 weeks out from an unplanned c section and have been really tired most days even though it feels like I am getting enough sleep (I go to bed at like 10 and usually get up twice every night for LO but husband helps me sleep until 9am).   
> I am not sure if it’s from the progesterone only birth control I have been on for the last couple of weeks or the c-section still healing. I still have some pain around my incision and swelling throughout the day as well.

I’m struggling with feeling dizzy sometimes and I don’t have much of an appetite, which is something I’ve never struggled with. I feel so guilty because my husband is having to do so much to help me and I just feel so useless. I tried to go for a very quick walk today and couldn’t even manage this. I’m so exhausted and keep crying because I can’t cope with it.

**[pbu11-0118]**

> Fast forward to 2.5 months ago I just had my first baby whom I adore. No regrets. This munchkin is the coolest person I've ever met! I barely could move. I also carried low and my baby was head down from the beginning of third trimester. My OB always said to not worry about it. Once I gave birth I lost 18lb in the first 2 weeks... and now I am stuck carrying the extra 22lb which is killing me. Nothing fits. nothing. I can't look at my closet without sobbing and I know this time around my body will be even more distorted than before. It's triggering honestly. I am breastfeeding as well so I know I can't diet. I eat normally but I have not lost any more weight. I had a c-section and don't feel good enough to workout yet. I cry too much about this despite the fact that I know at least this time around I got a cute baby out of it. It's just really bringing back how terrible I felt before.

### 21. The help plan depends on trust, culture, actual work and changing needs; relatives can withdraw help, demand access or be deliberately kept outside the village.

**Current findings section:** §3 stock comments; §5 relatives; §6 visitors; §10 helper plan; §17.4 help ending.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends.**

Current wording: “Helpers vs visitors is the distinction she draws:”

**What should change / interpretation:** Keep the distinction but add whether the proposed helper is trusted at all, what happens if help is conditional or withdrawn, and how family history or culture limits generic boundary advice. A no-contact instruction can be unwanted, and a visitor can be welcome. Pet safety, bereavement and housing also alter who can help. Do not treat every offer as usable capacity.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu11-0063]**

> So ultimately I’ve decided I need to go no contact and potentially low contact in the future with my mother and stepfather. After decades of emotional unavailability, especially through my two miscarriages this year, it’s just time. I’m having nightmares, becoming physically sick and my mental health is TRASH from the treatment lately and I have to break this decades old toxic cycle we have fallen into.

> Our souls know each other. An old soul for sure.
> 
> God bless all of you.”
> 
> Maybe it’s just pregnancy hormones, but I am sooo annoyed over this all 😂 I’m honestly thinking I should do some curb walking, eat spicy foods, go for a drive in a bumpy road, whatever to get this baby to come now or find a way to make sure baby stays in until my due date!

**[pbu11-0196]**

> There’s only 2 people in my family that never abused me that I feel I can trust with my child. I removed myself from family group chats and stopped answering calls from my extended family. But now I’m worried on how hard my child’s life would be with just me and him. I’m scared I’m setting him up to never know his other cousins and being the weird kid with no social skills. I am 1000% don’t want to put him in harms way. But I really don’t make enough money to not get help, and I know those 2 people will probably burn out quick if I solely rely on them.
> 
> Is anyone else going through a period where they’re really considering making their village small on purpose? Is this really the best idea or should I just keep a tight leash while letting them help me? I just want to do what’s best for my kid and I’m nervous about each option.

**[pbu11-0223]**

> Since finding out I was pregnant, I’ve had to move twice and now live in a country where I don’t speak the language. We’re dealing with ongoing immigration issues and there’s a real chance we’ll have to move again six months after the baby is born. Right now we’re living with my partner’s mum, who is very critical of my eating habits and honestly triggers a lot of feelings from my own difficult relationship with my mother. The house we’re supposed to move into won’t be ready for another month or two, so I’ll be moving again while heavily pregnant.
> 
> I keep trying to learn the language, but I can’t seem to focus with everything else going on.
> 
> My partner is here and involved, but the pregnancy was unplanned and we were long-distance when it happened. He’s unemployed and under a lot of stress trying to sort everything out. Lately he’s been telling me that me and the baby are the root of all his problems and that he can’t keep living like this.

**[pbu02-0019]**

> My husband wants to continue training and management until the baby arrives before making any major decisions.  
> I feel like the current level of stress, unpredictability, and recent escalation is not sustainable for me during pregnancy or postpartum.
> 
> He feels I am making an ultimatum about rehoming and says he would not give up his dog under pressure. And at this point it’s severely damaging our marriage and hurting the foundation we need before our first baby.

**[pbu02-0052]**

> Now, with my pregnancy, she is an incredibly excited grandmother. She has been involved in the baby shower and has even come to stay with me so she can help care for us during the first month postpartum. This is a level of involvement and care I could never have imagined receiving from her, at least until I was around 28.

**[pbu02-0053]**

> However, I have also been transparent with him about my trauma, which has become particularly present as I transition into motherhood. I sometimes wonder how I will know how to be a good mother when I did not have a healthy maternal relationship to learn from.

**[pbu02-0054]**

> She has no job, no money, is in debt, and truthfully we are all angry and dissapointed in her. She has very little to offer a baby, but she did not want an abortion so we are going to support her. 
> 
> I was hoping for some moms to share some advice or things they wish they knew when they had a newborn at home. I have been encouraging her to read, but I’m not sure if she actually is.

**[pbu02-0056]**

> All she would tell me is that early post partum, I “snatched” baby from her arms with a “very angry” look on my face. This was the infamous incident she has been punishing me for for months. That she isolated me for. That she turned my husband’s family against me for. That she revoked all support during the hardest most vulnerable time of my life for.

**[pbu02-0058]**

> Yesterday, my husband got incredibly mad at me because I was getting upset after everything I was doing to try to soothe my baby just wasn't working. He brought up names of people close to me and said I'd ruin my relationships with them all if I snap because they do something like touch my baby or ask too many questions or make comments.

**[pbu02-0059]**

> Nothing seems okay anymore unless I pretend to be happy and okay with everything.

**[pbu02-0129]**

> Our conversation consisted of her saying “Of course, because why would you want to talk to me for anything else” “I wouldn’t have come if I knew he was there. Why would I want to be around him?” “Good luck with him.” “Did you really expect that I’d be happy for you?” (No, but I thought maybe there was a chance because you’re my mother)

**[pbu02-0156]**

> Today i was really frustrated and just spoke very rudely making it clear that asking for a boy is torturing me and to through all the goddess photos from the house as she is disrespecting women. She is a little old and am worried that i spoke a bit rude. All her daughters say me to ignore her words as she is getting old. I really dont know how to ignore. Feeling a bit bad as spoke rudely but then who else can take a stand.

**[pbu02-0250]**

> But I have basically everything unasked now because she keeps going to free baby drives etc and picking stuff up without asking and giving it no matter how much I say its not needed and she needs to leave it to those who seriously do.   
> Shes even went as far as to give someone her old baby stuff who really desperately needs it but decided to be like ‘nah actually its going to my best friend’ the second we found out about this pregnancy.   
> I feel so so guilty and have gotten in touch with the woman to see if she wanted to keep it but she was so hurt by the action she said no.

**[pbu02-0269]**

> Aside from that, I’m nervous to tell family I’m pregnant because every time I come on this sub, I see horror stories about MILs, family, even partners treating the pregnant person poorly, as if the baby and pregnancy are “theirs” and not respecting the pregnant person’s boundaries. I have a good relationship with my family and in laws, but after reading the countless stories on here, I’m scared they’ll start acting crazy and I won’t be prepared. The stories about husbands taking the side of their family rather than pregnant wife also make me really sad. I don’t think my husband will do that, but I’m sure anyone who’s shared their story wasn’t expecting that either.

**[pbu02-0315]**

> All of a sudden, she‘s baby talking to me and asking me if she can come and hold my hand when I have the baby. I understand their love. but I just can’t get over the things that surfaced again in my first trimester.

**[pbu05-0013]**

> 3 weeks has passed by, my boyfriend’s mom has not asked how I feel or if we need anything for the baby. I am not from US, so I don’t know what should I expect from his family. In my country, when a woman is pregnant, the guys family will start to offer the help right away. They will ask if the woman has craving for certain food and they will pamper the woman to make sure she gets enough attention during pregnancy. I felt so far I got nothing from my boyfriend’s family and they don’t even ask how I feel during my pregnancy. I don’t know if I have unrealistic expectations right now

**[pbu05-0031]**

> Having my now 1 month old son made me realize how vulnerable babies are and how I could never leave him and I never want to be separated from him and it just brought on such a wave of emotions. I think having my own child was the final step at validating my own feelings about my parents. When I was fresh postpartum and very hormonal I kept having recurring thoughts that I can’t let my mother hold my baby because she will drop him and hurt him. Those thoughts have become less paranoid but I still have a lot of anxiety about her visit.

**[pbu05-0032]**

> I don’t know how to a) handle her visit well and set boundaries or b) tell her to postpone it. My husband says I should think about what’s best for my son and that it’s important for him to have a grandma, and that she will be helpful to us, but how can she be if I have all those anxieties about leaving her with my child? I just worry that we will either fight a lot or cry or she will be extremely upset if I tell her to postpone her visit.
> 
> I think I need to at least have some conversation with her about how I’m feeling, but I don’t know how to handle it and how to have it be productive.

**[pbu05-0142]**

> The hardest part of my postpartum experience was hands down my MIL, and not the newborn trenches. Since my baby was born, my MIL made comments like "I know more than you" not in a pretentious way but kind of joking. It always rubbed me the wrong way. When I asked her to do things differently from how she would do them (like sit baby up instead of bottle feeding her lying flat), she would begrudgingly do it but sigh and roll her eyes and not say a word. She pretty much rolled her eyes at everything. She told other family members that we thought we knew everything, and then of course they would tell us.

**[pbu05-0143]**

> I can't get over the eye rolling. I've been to therapy, I'm on medication and I'm doing so much better overall. But I can't let it go. People recommended no contact but they don't understand the cultural nuances at play here. I can't do that. There are huge cultural differences between us but she turned into a completely different person once my baby was born and the relationship we had before is gone. I still can't talk to her about it.
> 
> I still feel like a terrible mother. I haven't gotten over this. My husband is supportive and always stood up for me, but words of affirmation do not come naturally to him when I am struggling like this and want reassurance. I can't keep bringing this up with him but it still has a massive effect on me. We're talking about having a second child and idk if I can go through this again with her.

**[pbu05-0172]**

> I guess I'm nervous about when to tell them about the pregnancy, because I don't want them to think I have expectations of them.
> 
> Context: Our cousin also lived there for years with us, and had 5 or 6 babies that we, but eventually just they, have had to watch over the years. (She left them with her parents to go join bf again.) 
> 
> I know my sister in particular has experienced burnout for having to be responsible for those kids, and my 21 yr old brother is the family chauffeur despite being exhausted from military training. 
> 
> I really don't want them to associate my pregnancy, or becoming true uncles and auntie for the first time, with all of that. I want them to be happy, not stressed or skeptical. 
> 
> So my thought has been to keep it under wraps until I'm SUPER pregnant, so that they know I can take care of myself and won't be asking everything of them.
> 
>  I know this probably all sounds super insecure, and it probably is. I don't expect them to be SUPER EXCITED, they're kids! But it will break my heart if the burden associated with pregnancy makes them want to see me less.

**[pbu05-0177]**

> He says he’s willing to clear half the room and move things into the closet but it’s a small room to begin with. “Half” doesn’t feel like much, maybe enough space for a crib and a small chair. And when I picture it, all I can see is dust, clutter, and wires. I keep thinking… is this really where I’m going to put the baby?
> 
> At the same time, I feel guilty even feeling this way. I know he is making a sacrifice by giving up his space. He’s a genuinely kind person and I know he’ll be a great dad. I don’t want to hurt him or make him feel bad by criticizing his space.
> 
> But I’m also struggling with the idea of not having a clean, calm nursery like I imagined. I keep wondering, what if nothing really changes? What if the space never actually feels clean or safe? I’m trying not to spiral and maybe things will improve as we get closer to the baby’s arrival… but right now I just feel stuck between guilt and stress.

**[pbu05-0186]**

> When we had our first baby boy, my MIL showed up a day after the birth talking about coming to her first grandchild and being there for us blah blah blah. 
> 
> We did not have a nanny or househelp with baby #1, but me and hubby were okay taking care of ourselves. I thought "Sure MIL has kids so she'll know how to come and help us out and it's her first grandchild". 
> 
> Instead, she literally just sat there either watching me or glued to the tv all day. She cooked one meal (the first week my husband handled all meals mostly takeways for the 3 of us, thereafter me and my husband alternated the cooking and dishes), did not clean, wash any of the dishes she used or do baby laundry.
> 
> She would hold the baby for no more than 15 minutes and then give him back to me because baby pooped, was crying, fussy etc. She invited people over without asking us (she basically finished our wine collection) and I would clean up and dishes before my husband came back from work. 
> 
> All I wanted to do was recover in peace and she would talk to me endlessly expecting active conversations whenever I was in the living areas. Her being around like that completely pissed me off.

**[pbu05-0187]**

> Baby number two is due soon and MIL now is talking about coming to live with us 2 weeks before and when the baby is born to 'be there for the baby' and 'take care of me'. I told my husband it's a hard no and his mom must wait 2 weeks after birth to visit. My husband says he sees my point but 'it's his mother', and he and our now nanny will handle his mom (I want my nanny to focus on the toddler, meals and house) 
> 
> I want to tell MIL directly to wait two weeks (cue drama) because if my husband tells her I feel like she will still show up anyway. She is somewhat a nice person and I don't know if she lacks self-awareness or just doesn't respect boundaries, but I refuse to put myself in that position again. 
> 
> FYI, My mother passed away years ago and she was such a hands-on caring person but she also understood boundaries, so therefore I do not crave a mother or caretaker figure after giving birth.

**[pbu05-0190]**

> I decided before I even knew I was pregnant that I don't mind my family meeting my baby, I want my little brother to meet my baby, and my mom. But none of my parents will be left alone with the baby I can't trust it. My dad and step mom used to deliberately not tell my mom things that were happening to me, and because my mom didn't know and more happened. My dad also brushed a lot under the rug for his friend and it's just broken my trust with them. My step mom also used to be a very angry lady but she is getting a lot better.

**[pbu05-0279]**

> i am 13 weeks pregnant and just cut off contact w my mom and grandma. ever since i first found out i was pregnant they have done nothing but put more added stress on me, feel the need to share my pregnancy w people i barely even know, and team up against me.
> first red flag was me first finding out i was pregnant and telling my family i wanted to keep it a secret from everybody else until i was ready to announce; they decided to go tell people behind my back thinking it wouldn’t get to me. i let it go for the sake of them just being happy, then instead of addressing my baby as mine it’s “OUR child” for everything which was making me feel like this is not MY pregnancy.
>   today was the icing on the cake when my grandma called me asking if i would be able to take two strollers and i told her that i would most likely only need one, she then asked me if i had a baby walker and i told her yes my boyfriend’s sister got us one. she called my mom twisted the whole phone call up and said i refused everything she bought for my baby and i made her cry. my mom then texts my phone calling me “rude as fuck” i explained to her that i did not refuse any gifts and she bombards my phone with text messages calling me out of my name saying “she doesn’t give a fuck, i made her mom cry and i’m a rude ass person” so you know what?? i simply stated they will NOT be apart of my baby’s life for how they treated me during my pregnancy.

**[pbu05-0315]**

> Another night weeks later I fell asleep there after doing some cleaning and general stuff accidentally and it led to his dad threatening to kick my boyfriend out of the house and texting my boyfriends mom(they are divorced and rarely speak) that the company he keeps around is questionable and drug addicts(referring to me)- at this point I just found out I was pregnant and it was jarring for me and felt odd considering 1) my partner is an adult 2) his dad does not own the trailer 3) I have never drank alcohol or done any drugs. We have since got the place livable but renovations are still needed- some of the rooms still need flooring and we have to hang/replace doors and install some counters in the kitchen but overall it is livable and functional and coming together nicely. His dad recently found out I am pregnant and called my boyfriend and insisted we sell the trailer and rent a town house that would cost us an additional $900 a month an expense we can afford but it makes no sense when that money could go towards fixing up a place we own and pay less on. We do not plan on living in the manufactured home forever and after renovations are completed we intend to begin saving for a house and purchase in the next 5-6 years. The park we live in is safe, but it is a trailer park. I’m not entirely sure why it was even a suggestion and I’m slightly annoyed by it honestly. Overall after every experience I’ve with his dad make me uncomfortable and I don’t want him around me when I am most vulnerable after childbirth and feeling more protect over my newborn but my boyfriend wants his dad to have a monitored relationship with his father and is worried about the conflict and family drama not allowing his dad to see the kid will start. I also don’t have a reasonable excuse that doesn’t start drama so I feel a bit trapped.

**[pbu05-0317]**

> So I’ve felt pretty alone so far in this pregnancy I’m at 10 weeks now and finally starting to not be unbelievably nauseous and tired, I was having a particularly hard day and my mother in law checked in with me and no one else had been so I told her how I was I feeling. Well…she suggested I drink catnip tea and sent me a Amazon link (extremely dangerous for pregnant women and can cause miscarriages) and then invited me to join her for a “hoe down” I told her before this I was nauseous and my back hurt and I was mostly sleeping all day?!? She then went on to say that pregnancy isn’t even hard until month 9 so basically invalidating me and telling me that I shouldn’t feel bad in my first trimester, mind you she was much much younger than me for all her pregnancies so I also feel like yea this might be easier if I were 21 but I’m 37 so it’s not. She also thanked me for “carrying a baby for US” (referring to her and her son, MY husband?!) which I find weird like I’m a surrogate and they are the couple. My husband has her blocked on social media and only replies to her texts like once a month, we barely see her but I’m worried she will come around a lot once her first grandbaby is born and it stresses me out. Lastly she has a boyfriend who does meth and they both get in a weird amount of drunken fights and we just don’t want our baby around that sort of thing. I’ve told my husband a no contact situation may end up happening and to be prepared.

**[pbu05-0350]**

> Anyways we’ve been married for a little over a year now and we wanted a baby so I’m pregnant (yay!) to say the least they’re extremely unsupportive. When I called and told them they have never once said congratulations or offered to help or said anything supportive instead they said they were worried (about what I have no idea, we’re in an amazing spot to have a kid, not that they’d know because they gossip and assume about peoples lives instead of caring and helping others). My sister calls and tells me they’ve been tearing me apart behind my back which is the LEAST shocking thing, they told everyone, the day I married him, that my husband was abusive and I ran away and was trying to get pregnant since I met him?? Like a whole bunch of lies and once everyone met him were absolutely shocked my parents would say that about him or me (they called me manic and said I was going off the rails etc etc). Anyways my parents invite me to thanksgiving at their house (20 seconds before I told them I was pregnant) and then didn’t talk to me for a little bit and then decided I could still come. Well at this time all my family will be there (the only one I have issues with is my parents) and I thought that’d be a perfect time for the baby shower, everyone’s already in town and everyone could be included. My sister is excited and helping me plan the baby shower for the Saturday after thanksgiving, I give my family the tentative date so they can make sure they stay in town and can be there. My brother within 2 min says he can’t come because he has to go to his church on Sunday and has to leave Saturday morning (he lives 4 hrs away) my parents just straight up don’t answer, like the message or anything. But my dad was quick to post “***I’M*** a grandpa and ***I*** love ***MY*** first grandbaby” made it entirely about him and I think he did this to look like he’s a good dad who already knew and no one would gossip saying wow she didn’t tell her dad. Anyways. All this to say I don’t know what else to do. Cutting them off creates a very unnecessary divide between the family that I do care deeply about. But I don’t want them to see my baby. If they won’t support me pregnant then they won’t take pictures and pretend to love my baby. I just want to tell everyone the original plan won’t work out and just take it to my grandmas house and involve the people who care. Unfortunately doing that will not allow some people to be there that I wanted to be there but it sounds more enjoyable for me and less forced to make my parents happy.

**[pbu05-0353]**

> I enjoy my pregnancy so far, I'm 25 weeks today, healthy, gaining 40 lbs is the part I struggle the most with. My husband is great and supportive and assumes most of the mental load while I'm busy making our first baby to come earthside after 2 years of trying and 2 miscarriages.
> 
> But what is wrong with people?!?! Especially older women. From "Are you sure there's only one in there?!, "Wow, you're BIG!!!", judging the name we chose after THEY ASKED us what it was, wanting to buy a gift when we said there wasn't anything we needed (a lot of borrowed or second hand stuff from our friends which helped a ton!), giving the unborn child a ridiculous nickname, calling them "my" baby... The worst part happened yesterday when 2 people in my family tried to touch my belly and got upset when I said I didn't like being touched.
> 
> I'm usually very tough on my boundaries, but now I just feel helpless. Why?! Why are people so nosy about pregnancy, why do they feel entitled to a certain role in an unborn child's life, why do they insist on making these useless comments?!
> 
> That's all. I know it's common and that there's nothing to do besides standing our ground and setting our boundaries, but truly I'm just amazed at people's lack of respect.

**[pbu05-0422]**

> At first I was thinking about letting her know that I had a child after delivery by writing her a letter. In that letter I also wanted to let her know that she wasn’t going to be the grandmother, we already chose a safe and loving person to become the grandmother. But, we would give our child the option 18 to contact my “mother”. 
> 
> The reason for this is that she broke contact and cut everyone off from her family and friends when I was eight and I had a really difficult time with that (also because the abuse got worse). 
> 
> After talking to my therapist I decided that I have to do what’s best for my child and keep the child safe. So I have decided to no longer write her a letter. (side note: ) I have been talking a lot about the trauma that has surface since getting pregnant. I have nightmares in which my “mother” tries to steal my baby. She always told me that I was good for nothing but giving her grandchildren and that was my only purpose (besides caring for her). She is a woman with a lot of entitlement so my partner and I are scared that she will still show up at daycare or our house and we’re prepping for that case.

**[pbu05-0423]**

> My dad (divorced from my “mother” and understand that he isn’t allowed to share anything with her about me and our baby) wants me to let my sister know that I’m pregnant. I don’t know if I want that for two reasons. One, she feels like a stranger and often makes my stuff about her. Second, she is close with my “mother” and she hasn’t respect my boundaries in the past so I’m afraid she will share photos and stories with her. (possible three) my partner doesn’t think she will taking a role in our childs life because she is already so absent and he barely knows her. 
> 
> What would you do?
> 
> TL;DR: should I let my sister, who is in contact with my abusive mother, know that I’m pregnant?

### 22. Preparation needs include solo mothers, trans fathers and partners who are frightened, grieving or in crisis; couple-centered teaching can exclude the very person seeking support.

**Current findings section:** §5 other side; §9 segments; §17.4 classes/social connection.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends; challenges a universal couple-based format.**

Current wording: “Antenatal classes bought for the social side, and failing there.”

**What should change / interpretation:** Add active exclusion by introductions in couples, paired emotions and couple-swap exercises, plus age-matched solo-parent community requests. Fathers need explanations and support for their own fear, not only instructions to help her. A trans father asks about caring for a son. Keep a prospective father's desire for updates beside the mother's explicit no-contact request rather than assuming a shared preparation plan.

**Verbatim evidence:**

**[pbu11-0002]**

> This time we tried every “trick” to have a girl. We just found out it’s a boy. I’m devastated. I’m scared I’m going to resent my second son for not being a girl. I’ve been so miserable since finding out the news that it’s put a massive strain on my relationship with my wife, to the point where she’s thinking maybe we shouldn’t have this baby. As much as I didnt want another boy, I don’t think not having him is the way to go either. I love my wife so much, but it feels like any decision right now is taking a turn for the worst in our relationship

**[pbu05-0373]**

> And yet, we were talking divorce only 4 weeks after our son was born. This changed only a couple weeks ago, after many awful fights and hard conversations, but I think we’re finally a real family. He finally completely fell in love with our son while on vacation, and now he can’t get enough of him. So **an advice from me**: if you feel like your relationship was perfect up until this point and now suddenly your husband is an actual monster -give him some grace. Not a lot, but some. Be patient and understanding, even when you want to rip his head off. It’s easier for us to become mothers, our bodies are working for 9 months to prepare us for this. For men, their whole life literally gets thrown upside down in a couple of hours and you can either get a “I love my baby to death” version or “I hate all of you, leave me alone” version. But if his behaviour is not changing even after many fights and talks, and he’s still not coming around as the baby gets bigger -you don’t need him.

It was not easy for both of us and I’ve said some awful things too.
> 
> If I’m being perfectly honest all this talk about leaving your husbands at the first opportunity I see only on reddit. Literally every single real life couple I know has been through hell the first year, constantly on the bridge of divorce and saying things that should’ve never been said because of how tired and sleep deprived they were. And now they’re thriving.
> 
> All I’m saying is: if you KNOW your partner and you SEE that he’s struggling, give him time and be patient. If it’s not changing or getting worse, leave. I would’ve leaved if my partner didn’t get better. People love throwing words like abuse nowadays. Sometimes it’s just good people that are having a literal mental breakdown and have no idea how to cope.
