### 23. Announcing a pregnancy is a support, grief and privacy decision, including how to tell someone who has lost a baby or avoid disclosure to an unsafe relative.

**Current findings section:** §3 others' reactions; §6 identity/visitors; §10 communication planning.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends.**

Current wording: “Visitors: rules written in pregnancy.”

**What should change / interpretation:** Add the earlier information-control work: telling a partner after loss, preserving support while uncertain, announcing privately to someone grieving, model texts, whether a sibling will pass information to an abusive parent, and unwanted workplace attention. Parents can want early support or a private secret. Public disclosure can also feel like pressure to continue.

### 24. Purchases and recommendations are judged against a very specific problem; a bought product can fail while the underlying goal succeeds, and free services can have an agenda.

**Current findings section:** §4 products/apps/classes; §8 money; §11 bias and value; §10 evidence for a guide.

**Relationship to current conclusion: extends.**

Current wording: “The commercial complaint is specific.”

**What should change / interpretation:** Extend outcome-promise skepticism to actual purchase sequences and service motives. The vaping-aid buyer calls the items wasted money but reports quitting; a prenatal exercise shopper specifies DR, back pain and PT; a private scan can bring clarity, loss confirmation or more fear; a free clinic can feel coercive. Prospective supplement totals are not completed purchases. These requests and adjacent purchases do not establish willingness to buy Mamivio.

>

>

**[pbu05-0206]**

> I recently started using the app Food Recalls to check for recent listeria outbreaks, and was shocked to see that, in the last year, **prepared pasta was linked to more cases of death and fetal loss than any of the foods doctors commonly tell you to avoid**.
> 
> This includes items like cold pasta salad and pre-made pasta meals that you heat up in the microwave. **Microwaved food is especially risky because they do not heat food evenly**; there are often cold pockets where some of the food does not reach a high enough temperature to kill of listeria. While they didn't have any hospitalizations associated with them, other microwaved food like breakfast burritos had documented listeria outbreaks last year.
> 
> In fact, the pasta was the only type of food linked to fetal loss at all in the last year in the US.
> 
> I wanted to put this out there so people know to **discuss with your doctor** at your next appointment. I actually almost bought a big container of pasta salad early in my pregnancy because I was struggling to find foods to eat, and I'm glad I didn't. I will be avoiding it going forward.

**[pbu05-0207]**

> * Due to the current political climate in the US, there is a risk that not all outbreaks will be reported or accurately documented. The app links a source for each outbreak or update, but does not give a comprehensive list of where they usually get their data.
> * Without a proper study, it's difficult to compare the risk of each type of food. I have a looooot of questions about this. Are there fewer deli meat-related hospitalizations because it's more well known and people are more likely to avoid it during pregnancy? Or have our safety standards improved due to previous outbreaks? Would love to see a study on this!
> * I am not a doctor, so I recommend discussing this with yours!

> I’m 12 weeks and im certain now is not the time for my first child. My OBGYN asked me to get an ultrasound after a blood test to determine how many weeks i was because i had no idea when my last period was (mine are super irregular). So i found this place that does free ultrasounds and the google reviews looked good. Did not realize it was not at all a “pro-choice” institution.
> 
> They started off with a “consultation” which was really just me locked in a room with this woman who spent 20 minutes shaming me about my choice to abort, sharing a bunch of scary info about the risks of abortion including misinformation, countering every reason i had for my choice, going over all these details about the stage of the baby’s development, asking judgmental questions and going over these pamphlets with me about how shitty aborting would make me feel.

**[pbu05-0228]**

> Then came the actual ultrasound. The “consultation” lady went in with me for some fucking reason. & here i was thinking I’d be given the choice about whether or not to look. I tried not to but it felt like when she & the nurse noticed it they would always be like “oh look at \_\_\_” etc, pointing out every detail of the fetus etc. for some reason he was very active today and i saw him move a bunch, kicking around his little legs and everything. It was so hard to keep myself from sobbing but they kept going and kept asking me about how this made me feel and things like that. Then they just like took the paper cover off my lower half before i had pulled my underwear and pants back up and the creepy consultation lady saw my crotch and everything. She did not look away. It was humiliating. They then brought out this little model of how big he was by now and gave me photos of the ultrasound, and having seen the baby by then I couldn’t bring myself not to take them, even though I knew it was probably a bad decision for my mental health.

**[pbu05-0229]**

> I was perfectly comfortable with my decision to abort before this, even if slightly sad in small moments. But now I feel utterly destroyed and heartbroken. The pre-emptive grief comes in these huge waves. Seeing him made him feel like mine. And every second I just think about the fact that he’s still inside me, still with me, with tiny hands and feet, growing and innocent and never having asked to be made. And I will have to say goodbye. The grief makes me nauseous. Before this I would get slight tinges of sadness thinking about who it could become. But I was okay. Now after seeing him I don’t care who he’d be, he just IS and I’d love him regardless. And I’ll have to say goodbye. I’m so depressed. 
> 
> I know it was the goal of this clinic to make me feel this way and it was deeply cruel. But they succeeded in that goal. good for fucking them I guess. But I can’t change it and this is where im at now. im so heartbroken and angry. & I just was wondering if anyone had been through something similar.

> 
> I guess I’m just in some weird denial that this pregnancy is even real. Like I won’t actually believe that nothing is wrong without concrete proof of a heartbeat. **Further supporting IDs:** [pbu05-0014] [pbu05-0200] [pbu05-0220] [pbu05-0232] [pbu05-0290] [pbu05-0303] [pbu05-0308] [pbu05-0321] [pbu05-0327]

## Outliers and counter-examples

These qualify the patterns above. They are not prevalence estimates, universal outcomes or grounds to discount the difficult accounts. Repeated full passages retain their existing IDs and are counted once in coverage.

### A later pregnancy and birth after repeated loss can end in relief and a newborn at home.

**[pbu02-0170]**

> Even when I got pregnant this time, I wasn’t immediately hopeful. I had more information about my condition and followed everything closely like taking daily aspirin and heparin injections but emotionally, I stayed guarded. I simply did my best, surrendered and hoped. And now, I’m here, watching my newborn as I type this. It still feels surreal. I delivered through C section as it was the safer option for us.

**[pbu05-0012]**

> I was really sad at first, but now I am so happy to have a healthy baby girl. They said she is literally perfect! I have an anterior placenta, and I am about 225 pounds 5’5. So I haven’t popped yet or really felt her much yet, but oh boy! Is she a diva!!  At the end of the ultrasound, we got a picture of her throwing up a peace sign with her other thumb in her mouth and her little legs crossed! This is definitely my daughter😆. Now I just can’t wait to meet my beautiful girl💕. It’s okay to be disappointed at first, but I am just soooo blessed I have a healthy baby. When I thought I couldn’t even have kids again.

### The hoped-for improvement can arrive, including a substantially easier second pregnancy.

**[pbu02-0200]**

> So I’m 16 weeks now and MAN I feel so much better. In my first trimester I was sure I was going to end my life or cry myself into a miscarriage. But something in the last 2 weeks has just leveled me out. I have so much more energy and I feel like my mood is so stable. I know this is all old news but seriously my behavior and mood swings in the first trimester were on a level of bipolar disorder, psychotic breakdown, lay in bed for days on end crying, writing suicide notes, I called the suicide hotline multiple times and almost check myself into a psych ward. I hated myself and I was convinced everyone else hated me too and now I’m just fine? I’m just okay now?

**[pbu05-0434]**

> I am 14 weeks along into my second pregnancy. All the way through the first trimester, and I can say with confidence that I had a much easier time with it. Sure, I’ve had a couple rough days here and there but it does not hold a candle to my first. And the second I turned 14 weeks, any nausea I did have completely went away! Last time, it didn’t fully fizzle out until 20 weeks. I’m just so glad it was easier this time. So for anyone who is having a rough go around with their first and are wondering if you can even handle a second one, I wanted to offer some reassurance. 💕

**[pbu11-0351]**

> Guys. I'm 13 weeks in a couple of days and I just have to say it does get better. I didn't think it would but I'm feeling more like myself and my energy is somewhat better (still loving a nap) we got our NIPT test back this morning and all low risk and a BABY BOY!! I'm feeling like I can be excited now. And I'm ready to announce. Hang in there this is such a wild ride. 

### Pregnancy can be loved and missed even with loss history and ongoing anxiety.

### More testing is not always a compulsion: a positive test or scan can restore joy, and a Doppler can be enjoyed as connection.

Hopefully my ferritin levels have went up since I started taking an extra supplement. 🤞🏻 My blood pressure is good but sometimes anxiety causes my diastolic to be in in the high 80s/low 90s. They checked it at the end of the appointment and it was great. She wants me to start a baby aspirin and we’ll keep an eye on the blood pressure with office visits and at home monitoring. She used a doppler at the end of the appointment and his heart rate is 155bpm. I’m so happy that she seems so knowledgeable and helpful. Just excited that my baby boy is doing well and that I can put some of my worried to rest!

**[pbu05-0402]**

> Once I was under thier care I felt way more relaxed they looked at everything and said that this can just happen sometimes. The baby’s heart shape and chambers looked fine it’s the electrical pulses that can irregulate. Having had that in depth scan at 32 weeks which I took myself and made happen (that I should have had at 28 weeks as soon as they picked it up but didn’t send me to the fetal cardio specialist) this is where I could finally calm down abit. They said that approaching the 40 week mark the beats would more than likely regulate and once born would go back to normal normal.
> 
> This is exactly what happened with my boy ! He was monitored the minute he came out and every 3 hours for the next 2 days and everytime the reassured me it was fine. I just wanted to share this with you as some hospitals don’t have any experience with this and it’s more common than you think. Stay strong and try to calm down I cried everyday for the remainder of my pregnancy because the midwife’s had no clue about the condition. All the best with your pregnancies my baby is now 3 months and very much healthy xxx

### A current caring partner can disagree about continuation without being described as coercive.

> 
> I had an ultrasound yesterday, and for a brief moment there was concern that something might be wrong. The second I thought that might be the case, I completely crumbled. I cried, panicked, and felt a level of fear and sadness that honestly surprised me. That reaction forced me to confront feelings I had been trying not to think about.
> 
> Then, when everything turned out to be okay, the overwhelming sense of relief hit me just as hard. I can’t even fully explain it. In that moment, I realized how attached I had already become and how much I wanted everything to be okay. That experience made it impossible for me to keep pretending I was emotionally detached from all of this.

### Abortion is not followed by one universal feeling; some describe later satisfaction or no personal regret.

### An overall positive retrospective includes both parents' mental-health difficulty and rejects immediate leave-him advice.

**[pbu05-0372]**

> \- My baby never had any issues with differentiating day from night, so he was sleeping soundly from night 1. The first month was the hardest, cause he was only starting to learn how to drink from a bottle, so I had to be up for an hour, 3 times a night, cause it took him forever to finish.   
> \- I can’t remember more than 5 times that I had to rock or hold him to sleep. I just always put him in his crib, gave him a paci and stayed around for 10-15 minutes so he would fall asleep.

**[pbu05-0373]**

> And yet, we were talking divorce only 4 weeks after our son was born. This changed only a couple weeks ago, after many awful fights and hard conversations, but I think we’re finally a real family. He finally completely fell in love with our son while on vacation, and now he can’t get enough of him. So **an advice from me**: if you feel like your relationship was perfect up until this point and now suddenly your husband is an actual monster -give him some grace. Not a lot, but some. Be patient and understanding, even when you want to rip his head off. It’s easier for us to become mothers, our bodies are working for 9 months to prepare us for this. For men, their whole life literally gets thrown upside down in a couple of hours and you can either get a “I love my baby to death” version or “I hate all of you, leave me alone” version. But if his behaviour is not changing even after many fights and talks, and he’s still not coming around as the baby gets bigger -you don’t need him.

**[pbu05-0376]**

> My partner was diagnosed with PPD and was not himself at all, but he wanted to heal and change and was aware of his mistakes and behaviour. On top of a newborn he had a new very stressful job as a cmo in a new company, was the only family provider and had a wife that couldn’t take care of a baby because of her own mental struggles. It was not easy for both of us and I’ve said some awful things too.
> 
> If I’m being perfectly honest all this talk about leaving your husbands at the first opportunity I see only on reddit. Literally every single real life couple I know has been through hell the first year, constantly on the bridge of divorce and saying things that should’ve never been said because of how tired and sleep deprived they were. And now they’re thriving.
> 
> All I’m saying is: if you KNOW your partner and you SEE that he’s struggling, give him time and be patient. If it’s not changing or getting worse, leave. I would’ve leaved if my partner didn’t get better. People love throwing words like abuse nowadays. Sometimes it’s just good people that are having a literal mental breakdown and have no idea how to cope.

### Seeking treatment despite a custody threat did not produce the feared custody outcome in one author's account.

**[pbu11-0073]**

Post partum, I had my ex take our baby while I got treatment. He threatened to use it to remove custody from me, although he never ended up doing so, but i went to the hospital anyways and was honest with him about the PPD (later confirmed PPP). He didnt end up using it to take custody from me, but i fully thought he would and I put my baby first anyways. The doctors never called CPS on me, but my thoughts never escalated beyond wanting to hurt myself.

## What fits nowhere

These subjects need an explicit place in the rewrite. They are not off-niche leftovers: the proposed destinations in the patterns above should become named subsections where necessary.

### Continuation, abortion, contraception and access deserve a reproductive-decision section, not only birth preferences.

The teen's request for DIY abortion and the travel-related search for home methods are evidence of desperation and perceived access, not methods supplied by this digest. The sixteen-year-old's account is research evidence; age does not establish a customer segment. Existing §9's earlier absence of minors cannot be carried forward as absence of their voices.

Keep assault disclosure separate from infidelity assumptions, and paternity uncertainty separate from a week-by-week fetal-development question.

They took him back and said that his kidneys were not functional and slowly after he passed. My manager then took me room to room to find out who okayed the medication because obviously I wasn’t ready at all. We never found out. So I ran. I found myself at a hiking trail in the desert. Throughout all of this I could feel the grief in my chest and could hear the echos of people saying their condolences to me. When I got to the end of the trail I saw some people my dream person knew who were not nice. I bumped into their soda and then the group all ganged up on me. ### A non-parent educational/promotional clinical case needs an explicit provenance home.

The former is an advisor's anonymised clinical narrative, not first-person parent testimony or a verified treatment effect; the latter is a public-health announcement, not a parent's request or lived outcome.

> close monitoring.
> 
> Treatment was started based on her clinical findings, and she was followed throughout the pregnancy.
> 
> Then came the milestones  
> that had previously seemed out of reach.
> 
> The **pregnancy continued.**
> 
> Week after week, the baby kept growing.
> 
> And eventually, after five previous losses, she delivered her second child through a normal vaginal  
> delivery.
> 
> What made this pregnancy different wasn't simply good fortune. It was the decision to investigate the history of recurrent pregnancy loss and manage the pregnancy accordingly.
> 
> Recurrent pregnancy loss can have different underlying causes, which is why repeated  
> losses deserve proper medical evaluation rather than being dismissed as bad  
> **luck.**

## Requests

This index separates direct resource, product, plan, list, care and support requests from general reassurance. It also retains requests already made to professionals and services sought but not obtained. None is an offer to buy Mamivio. Brief descriptions below are interpretation; the linked IDs retain the parent’s wording in the evidence above or in the further-ID trail.

### UR11: direct requests

- [pbu11-0012] — Whether current prescribed treatment is enough or another medication should be discussed.
- [pbu11-0013] — Matching risk-result experiences and what clinicians prescribed.
- [pbu11-0018] — Symptoms to monitor and whether to call triage again.
- [pbu11-0020] — A pregnancy-compatible wedding dress and advice about the wedding day.
- [pbu11-0041] — An individualized medical opinion on a proposed supplement stack after online and Claude research.
- [pbu11-0048] — Questions for the first scan/gynecologist and a same-stage pregnancy companion.
- [pbu11-0054] — Help disentangling medication cessation, wanting a child and opposing therapy goals.
- [pbu11-0057] — Help resisting pressure to stop contraception shortly after C-section.
- [pbu11-0069] — Help for PPD; reports a CPS referral and subsequent refusal to speak to professionals.
- [pbu11-0073] — Psychiatric treatment despite an ex-partner's custody threat; describes obtaining it.
- [pbu11-0076] — Medication access and childcare that would make therapy possible.
- [pbu11-0086] — A request for earlier induction tied to avoiding surgical recovery while caring for older children.
- [pbu11-0087] — Practical instructions for first self-injection; partner is also frightened.
- [pbu11-0097] — Questions and preparation for an unexpectedly early NHS appointment.
- [pbu11-0098] — A birth-options comparison, induction versus planned C-section.
- [pbu11-0099] — Explanations during the scan and fetal-sex information; reports asking.
- [pbu11-0106] — A birth debrief and answers before discharge; regrets not receiving one.
- [pbu11-0112] — Useful initial GP care and a medication plan after being told to abort and stop medication.
- [pbu11-0117] — A coordinated birth plan between specialist consultants and midwives.
- [pbu11-0144] — Genetic testing while weighing a relative's privacy and effects on family planning.
- [pbu11-0146] — Access to special formula and consistent clinical follow-up.
- [pbu11-0147] — A prescribed product from the pharmacy that remains unavailable.
- [pbu11-0149] — More urgent mental-health/GP care than the offered wait.
- [pbu11-0151] — Ways to support an older child's relationship with his pregnant mother.
- [pbu11-0154] — A way to explain miscarriage/death to a five-year-old.
- [pbu11-0159] — Ideas for a persistent cough after equipment and multiple treatments failed.
- [pbu11-0173] — Whether to see a regular doctor before the midwife's first appointment.
- [pbu11-0174] — Supplements or nighttime routines to improve sleep after checking with the doctor.

- [pbu11-0193] — A screenshot/location of fetal-sex results so risk information can be read without spoiling the reveal.

## Verification and coverage

The check decodes the three assigned JSONL files, checks each reproduced evidence block against the quote field of the same ID, checks every ID anywhere in this digest against that share, checks all 25 quoted current-conclusion fragments against findings.md, and accounts for every source record. The check treats complete fields and literal excerpts separately; repeated passages count once by ID for coverage.

Programmatic result: 829 reproduced evidence blocks checked; 0 verbatim mismatches; 0 unknown IDs; 25/25 current-conclusion fragments matched. 592 unique records have their complete quote field reproduced; 40 additional unique records have literal excerpts. 147 quote records are in the money inventory. Source counts: 350 + 435 + 378 = 1,163.

Coverage: quotes in the share: 1,163; quotes reproduced verbatim: 632 unique IDs (592 complete fields + 40 excerpt-only IDs); further IDs listed: 531 unique IDs not reproduced; IDs not mentioned at all: 0.
